  READ IT: one line a part, 16 sixteenths a bar. `*` is a strike, `-` is the
  note still sounding, `.` is silence.

  Then every note, in the order it is struck, as  NAME@step:length
      A3@0:16    A3, struck on step 0, sounding sixteen sixteenths
      E5@8:4     E5, struck on step 8, sounding four
      C#2@0:24>  still sounding at the bar line — the grid stops, the note does not
  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.

  READ IT: one line a part, 16 sixteenths a bar. `*` is a strike, `-` is the
  note still sounding, `.` is silence.

  Then every note, in the order it is struck, as  NAME@step:length
      A3@0:16    A3, struck on step 0, sounding sixteen sixteenths
      E5@8:4     E5, struck on step 8, sounding four
      C#2@0:24>  still sounding at the bar line — the grid stops, the note does not
  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.

  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.

  READ IT: one line a part, 16 sixteenths a bar. `*` is a strike, `-` is the
  note still sounding, `.` is silence.

  Then every note, in the order it is struck, as  NAME@step:length
      A3@0:16    A3, struck on step 0, sounding sixteen sixteenths
      E5@8:4     E5, struck on step 8, sounding four
      C#2@0:24>  still sounding at the bar line — the grid stops, the note does not
  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.

  READ IT: one line a part, 16 sixteenths a bar. `*` is a strike, `-` is the
  note still sounding, `.` is silence.

  Then every note, in the order it is struck, as  NAME@step:length
      A3@0:16    A3, struck on step 0, sounding sixteen sixteenths
      E5@8:4     E5, struck on step 8, sounding four
      C#2@0:24>  still sounding at the bar line — the grid stops, the note does not
  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.

  READ IT: one line a part, 16 sixteenths a bar. `*` is a strike, `-` is the
  note still sounding, `.` is silence.

  Then every note, in the order it is struck, as  NAME@step:length
      A3@0:16    A3, struck on step 0, sounding sixteen sixteenths
      E5@8:4     E5, struck on step 8, sounding four
      C#2@0:24>  still sounding at the bar line — the grid stops, the note does not
  A chord shares one grid row, so the step and the length are the only way
  to see what each of its notes actually did. A number in brackets is how
  many milliseconds the note sits off its step — the groove, printed rather
  than hidden.
